<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Bose–Einstein condensation in every ground state of a dilute three-dimensional hard-sphere Bose gas. At every sufficiently small fixed gas parameter, the constant orbital contains a positive fraction of the particles in the thermodynamic limit. The fraction can be chosen independently of the gas parameter, and the conclusion holds for arbitrary complex ground states.

- **claim-fixed-parameter-condensation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): There are absolute constants $\varepsilon_0,c_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $a,\rho>0$ satisfying $\rho a^3<\varepsilon_0$. For every sequence of particle numbers $N_k$ and lengths $L_k$ such that $$N_k\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad L_k\longrightarrow\infty, \qquad \frac{N_k}{L_k^3}\longrightarrow\rho,$$ and every choice of normalized, possibly complex, bosonic ground eigenvectors $\Psi_k$ of the hard-sphere Hamiltonians, $$\label{main:limit} \liminf_{k\to\infty} B(\Psi_k)\ge c_0.$$
  - Explanation: The constant orbital has a uniform positive limiting occupation for every complex bosonic ground vector at sufficiently small fixed gas parameter.
- **claim-ground-space-mixtures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-mixed)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>, the same lower bound holds for every sequence of density operators supported on the bosonic ground eigenspaces. In particular, it holds for the normalized ground-space projections $\Pi_k/\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\Pi_k$, and for every sequence of normalized nonnegative ground functions.
  - Explanation: Linearity of occupation extends the uniform pure-state bound to density operators supported on the ground eigenspace.
- **claim-ground-component-phases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-ground)): For every integer $n\ge1$ with nonempty $\Omega_n$, the full and bosonic Dirichlet operators have compact resolvent and the same bottom energy $E_n$. In particular, both $\Psi$ and $\Phi=\|\Psi\|$ are full ground eigenfunctions at energy $E_N$. They have smooth representatives in $\Omega_N$. On every connected component $C$ of $\Omega_N$, either both vanish identically or $$\Phi>0,\qquad \Psi=e^{i\vartheta_C}\Phi$$ for one constant $\vartheta_C\in\mathbb R$. For every real bounded Lipschitz function $u$ on $\Lambda_K^N$, $$\label{setup:multiplier} q_N[u\Phi]-E_N\lVert u\Phi\rVert_2^2 =\int\Phi^2\|\nabla u\|^2.$$ The gradient on the right includes all particle coordinates.
  - Explanation: The full and bosonic ground energies agree. The modulus remains a ground eigenfunction, and each positive component has a constant phase.
- **claim-allowed-sobolev-slices** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-slices)): For almost every fixed bath $Y$, a function $f\in H_0^1(\Omega_N)$ restricts to an element of $H_0^1(S_Y)$, where $$S_Y=\{x\in\Lambda_K:(x,Y)\in\Omega_N\}.$$ The same assertion holds when any nonempty subset of coordinates is left unfixed. For the smooth ground representatives, every connected subset of $S_Y$ has one common phase on its nonzero points, or the wave function vanishes throughout it.
  - Explanation: Compactly supported approximation gives the slice form domain without requiring a regular boundary. Connected allowed slices inherit the component phase.
- **claim-phase-transfer-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-setup-phase-transfer)): Let $\{(x,Y):x\in U_Y\}$ be measurable. Suppose that for almost every bath with $m(Y)>0$, the set $U_Y$ is contained in one connected component of $S_Y$, or is empty. Define $$A_Y=\Lambda_K\setminus U_Y,\qquad \delta_{\rm geom}=\int m(Y)\frac{\|A_Y\|}{V}\,\,\mathrm dY.$$ Then $$\label{setup:phase-loss} B(\Psi)\ge B(\Phi)-4\sqrt{\delta_{\rm geom}}.$$ In particular, if $B(\Phi)\ge c_*>0$ and $\delta_{\rm geom}\le c_*^2/64$, then $B(\Psi)\ge c_*/2$.
  - Explanation: Discarding a small bath-averaged volume controls the occupation lost through phase disagreement outside a common slice component.
- **claim-cube-kinetic-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-cube)): Let $Q$ be a Euclidean cube of side $l$, and let $F\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever $\|x_i-x_j\|\le r$ for some $i\ne j$. With no boundary condition on the outer faces, $$\label{sp:cube-bound} \sum_{i=1}^n\int_{Q^n}\|\nabla_iF\|^2 \ge c_{\rm cube}\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\int_{Q^n}\|F\|^2, \qquad c_{\rm cube}=\frac1{64\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for every torus form function $F\in H_0^1(\Omega_N)$ and every fixed label subset $I\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}$, $$\label{sp:cell-energy} q_I[F]\ge c_{\rm cube}r\int\sum_{v\in\mathcal L} n_v^I(n_v^I-1)\|F\|^2.$$ For $D\ge2$ this implies $$\label{sp:energy-lower} \frac{E_N}{N}\ge c_E\alpha, \qquad c_E=c_{\rm cube}/2.$$
  - Explanation: Ray integration and subdivision yield hard-core kinetic coercivity without outer boundary conditions, then a cellwise energy penalty and a ground-energy lower bound.
- **claim-close-neighbor-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-close-energy)): Let $0<r<s<1$, let $F\in H_0^1(\Omega_N)$, and fix a label set $I\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}$. Define $d_i^I=\min_{j\in I\setminus\{i\}}d(x_i,x_j)$, with value $+\infty$ when the minimizing set is empty. Then $$\label{sp:close-energy-bound} q_I[F]\ge c_{\rm near}\,rs^{-3} \int\#\{i\in I:d_i^I\le2s\}\|F\|^2, \qquad c_{\rm near}=\frac{27}{262144\sqrt3}.$$
  - Explanation: A translated-grid average converts short nearest-neighbor distances into a kinetic penalty for the selected labels.
- **claim-normalized-particle-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-sp-insertion)): Let $w$ be a real normalized smooth one-particle orbital and $F$ an $n$-particle form function. For every configuration $X$ in the support of $F$, suppose at most $k$ old positions lie within distance $2r$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}w$. If $$\label{sp:insertion-smallness} C\lVert w\rVert_\infty^2kr^3\le\frac14,$$ there is an admissible function $\widetilde F$ with one more coordinate, having the same norm as $F$, such that $$\label{sp:insertion-cost} q_{n+1}[\widetilde F]\le q_n[F] +C\left(\lVert\nabla w\rVert_2^2+\lVert w\rVert_\infty^2kr\right)\lVert F\rVert_2^2.$$ The constants depend only on a fixed radial cutoff profile.
  - Explanation: Normalizing the new orbital separately at each old configuration preserves the old marginal and prevents a multiplicative error in the old kinetic energy.
- **claim-removal-energy-and-monotonicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-removal)): For all sufficiently large $D$, uniformly in the stated $\alpha$ window, $$\label{sp:removal-bound} E_N-E_{N-m}\le C\alpha m\qquad(0\le m\le N).$$ Moreover $E_n/n$ is nondecreasing among positive particle numbers with nonempty form domain, at fixed $K,r$.
  - Explanation: Repeated normalized insertion bounds removal gaps. Averaging label-subset energies proves monotonicity of ground energy per particle.
- **claim-joint-deficient-cell-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-low-counts)): One can fix $\alpha_0$ sufficiently large and then $\ell>0$ sufficiently small so that, for every $A\subset\mathcal L$ and all sufficiently large $D$, $$\label{sp:low-count-bound} \mu\{n_v<\ell D\text{ for every }v\in A\} \le (C/D^2)^{\|A\|}.$$ These choices and the constant $C$ are independent of $K$ and of the ground function.
  - Explanation: A deficiency energy penalty and products of count cutoffs give a joint low-count estimate for arbitrary prescribed cell sets.
- **claim-weighted-local-energy-penalty** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-localized-form)): There are constants $C,c>0$, independent of $b$, such that every $N$-particle form function $F$ satisfies $$\label{sp:localized-form-bound} q_N[F]-E_N\lVert F\rVert_2^2 \ge\alpha\int\bigl(f_A-CD\|A\|+cb^{-3}k_A\bigr)\|F\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Particle localization retains the outside ground energy while using the local kinetic energy to pay removal costs and leave count and close-neighbor penalties.
- **claim-joint-spatial-moments-and-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-sp-joint-tails)): For every $A\subset\mathcal L$, $$\label{sp:count-mgf} \log\int e^{zf_A}\,\mathrm d\mu\le CzD\|A\|\qquad(0\le z\le1),$$ where $f_\varnothing=0$. Fix any $\theta>0$. One can choose $b>0$ sufficiently small depending on $\theta$, then take $D$ sufficiently large, so that the random site set $$\label{sp:close-bad-set} \mathcal Q(X)=\left\{v\in\mathcal L: \#\{i:d(x_i,v)\le5,\ d_i\le s_0\}\ge\theta D\right\}$$ satisfies $$\label{sp:close-tail} \mu\{A\subset\mathcal Q\}\le p_D^{\|A\|},\qquad p_D=\left(C_{\theta}b^{-2}D^{-4/3}\right)^{1/Q}\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $Q$ is an absolute integer; $D$ is taken large enough that the displayed base is at most one. All statements are uniform for $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$, $K\ge100$, and the ground function.
  - Explanation: The multiplier identity controls exponential spatial counts. Separated close-count cutoffs and lattice coloring extend a joint rare-event bound to arbitrary site sets.
- **claim-stationary-killed-path-law** (construction, [source](#stmt-path-slab)): Equation <a href="#path:law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="path:law">[path:law]</a> defines a probability measure. Every time marginal is $\mu$. Its restriction to an interval of length $s$ has the same construction with endpoint multiplier $e^{sE_N}\Phi(X_-)\Phi(X_+)$, and it is invariant under time reversal. Given both endpoint configurations, it is the product torus-bridge law conditioned on mutual hard avoidance. If the winding increments are also fixed, the underlying product consists of the corresponding Euclidean bridges.
  - Explanation: Ground-state endpoint weighting normalizes the killed Brownian law, gives stationary position marginals, and identifies its conditional bridge laws.
- **claim-selected-path-deletion** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-deletion)): Let $I\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}$ have cardinality $m$. If $h$ is a nonnegative measurable single-path functional invariant under reversal, then $$\label{path:deletion-bound} \mathbb E_{\mathcal R}\prod_{i\in I}h(\omega_i) \le e^{Ct\alpha m} \int\mu(\,\mathrm dY)\prod_{i\in I}\mathbb E^{Y_i}_{\rm free}h.$$ The free expectation uses a Brownian path of length $2t$.
  - Explanation: Dropping selected interactions and using reversal symmetry bounds selected path functionals by free expectations with a removal-energy cost.
- **claim-free-path-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-regularity)): For every fixed $q>0$ and fixed $t>0$, a free Brownian path fails regularity with probability at most $C_{q,t}D^{-q}$. The bound is uniform in its initial point, $K$, and the allowed $\alpha$-window. It also holds for a Euclidean bridge of duration $2t$ with bounded endpoint displacement, or for two independent Euclidean bridge legs of duration $t$ joined at a prescribed middle point, when the endpoints and middle point lie in a fixed bounded region. The bound and the required lower threshold on $D$ may depend on that region and on $t$; the numerical regularity threshold $C_{\rm reg}$ is fixed once for all these laws.* *If all three prescribed points lie within distance $3$ of a fixed site $v$, the probability that the two-leg path leaves the ball of radius $10$ about $v$ is at most $Ce^{-c/t}$, with numerical $c,C>0$.
  - Explanation: Gaussian oscillation tails and high moment estimates make the regularity failures arbitrarily polynomially small for the stated Brownian and bridge laws.
- **claim-free-visit-exponential-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-free-visits)): For every $\lambda>0$, $$\label{path:free-visit-bound} \mathbb E^y_{\rm free}\bigl[e^{\lambda J_A\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D}}-1\bigr] \le \zeta e^{-d(y,A)},\qquad \zeta=C_\lambda\lambda\varepsilon_{\mathcal D}^{1/3}.$$ Here $d(y,A)$ is the distance from $y$ to the set of site centers. The constants $C_\lambda$ are bounded when $\lambda$ ranges over a bounded interval.
  - Explanation: Unit-ball exit counts control the number of visited sites, while travel tails and the rare path event supply the spatial decay.
- **claim-interacting-visit-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-visit-moments)): If $e^{Ct\alpha}\zeta\le1$, then $$\label{path:visit-mgf} \mathbb E_{\mathcal R}\exp\left(\lambda\sum_i J_A(\omega_i)\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D}(\omega_i)\right) \le \exp\bigl(Ce^{Ct\alpha}\zeta D\|A\|\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Expanding the path product, applying selected-path deletion, and then using the spatial moment bound transfers a free rare-event estimate to the interacting sample.
- **claim-joint-certificate-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-path-certificates)): Fix any $p_1>0$. One can choose $H_1$ large, then $t>0$ sufficiently small, and then $D_0$ sufficiently large, such that $$\label{path:certificate-bound} \mathcal R\{v\text{ has no certificate for every }v\in A\} \le p_1^{\|A\|}$$ for every set $A$ of distinct sites, every $D\ge D_0$, and sufficiently large $K$. The bound is uniform over the chosen ground function and the allowed $\alpha$-window. After $H_1$ is fixed, $t$ may be reduced further before increasing $D_0$.
  - Explanation: Joint spatial tails and visit moments bound each certificate failure type, and a union over assigned failure types gives a joint cell estimate.
- **claim-averaged-bridge-gaussian-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-bridge-gaussian-packing)): Let $y_1,\ldots,y_m\in\mathbb R^3$ have pairwise distances at least $s_0$, where $m\ge\ell D/2$ and $s_0=bD^{-1/3}$. Fix $u\in\mathbb R^3$ and $0<s\le t/2$. The average of the time-$s$ densities of duration-$t$ Brownian bridges from $y_i$ to $u$ is bounded everywhere by $$\label{bridge:packed-density} C\left(1+\frac{1}{D s^{3/2}}\right).$$ Here $C$ depends on $b,\ell$, but not on $t,s,D$ or $u$.
  - Explanation: Separated Gaussian means and disjoint-ball packing bound bridge densities after averaging the endpoint labels.
- **claim-averaged-obstacle-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-bridge-averaged-hit)): Fix $m\ge\ell D/2$ pairs $(y_i,z_i)$ with $y_i\in B_v$ and $\|z_i-v\|\le3$. Assume that each of the two endpoint lists is $s_0$-separated. Let $c$ be a deterministic regular torus path whose outer endpoints are at distance greater than $s_0$ from the corresponding endpoints of every label under consideration. Then, for every target cell $B$, $$\label{bridge:averaged-estimate} \frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m Q_{y_i,z_i}^{B}(\mathcal C_v\cap\mathcal H(c)) \le Ctr+C\frac{rD^{3\gamma}}{D\sqrt{h_0}}.$$ The same conclusion holds if $c$ is the original path of one of these labels: omit that label’s summand and retain the divisor $m$, assuming endpoint separation for all the remaining labels. The constant $C$ may depend on $b,\ell,C_{\rm reg}$, but is independent of small $t$.
  - Explanation: Endpoint separation eliminates the outer windows. Averaged Gaussian densities and the regularity mesh bounds control collisions over the remaining intervals.
- **claim-single-replacement-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-bridge-single-hit)): Let $y\in B_v$, $\|z-v\|\le3$, and let $c$ be a deterministic regular path satisfying $d(y,c(0))>s_0$ and $d(z,c(2t))>s_0$. For either $Q=Q_{y,z}^{B}$, with any target cell $B$, or $Q=Q_{y,z}^{0}$, one has $$\label{bridge:single-estimate} Q(\mathcal C_v\cap\mathcal H(c)) \le Ctr+C\frac{rD^{3\gamma}}{\sqrt{h_0}} \le C\alpha\bigl(tD^{-1}+D^{-1/4}\bigr).$$ Thus this probability tends to zero with $D$ for each fixed $t$.
  - Explanation: Single-endpoint density bounds give a vanishing collision probability against a fixed regular obstacle under either stated bridge law.
- **claim-successful-label-target-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-bridge-success-supply)): Consider a collection of deterministic torus paths with endpoints $(y_j,z_j)$, and fix a cell $v$. Suppose that at most $H_1D$ of these paths visit the ball of radius $12$ about $v$, and that every visiting path is regular. Suppose a list of $m\ge\ell D/2$ labels satisfies $y_i\in B_v$, $\|z_i-v\|\le3$, and that both endpoints of every listed label are at distance greater than $s_0$ from every other endpoint in the corresponding full configuration.* *Call a trial for label $i$ successful if it satisfies $\mathcal C_v$ and avoids every obstacle path except the original path of $i$. For sufficiently small fixed $t>0$ and then sufficiently large $D$, at least $9m/10$ listed labels have the following property: for each of the seven target cells $B$, the set $$\label{bridge:successful-target-set} T_{i,B}=\{u\in B: Q_{y_i,z_i}^u(\text{successful trial})\ge9/10\}$$ has volume at least $99/100$. All choices are uniform in the deterministic collection, $v$, and $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$.
  - Explanation: The averaged collision estimate pays for all nearby obstacles; successive averaging thresholds retain most labels with large successful midpoint sets.
- **claim-certified-cell-eligible-labels** (bound, [source](#stmt-group-certified-labels)): At a cell with a certificate there are at least $\ell D/2$ eligible labels. After $t$ has been chosen sufficiently small and $D$ sufficiently large, at least nine tenths of these labels satisfy the following stronger property: for every target cell $B_w$, $$\label{group:full-success} \left\|\left\{u\in B_w: Q_i^u(C_i(F_i^{\rm all}))\ge0.9\right\}\right\|\ge0.99.$$ Here $F_i^{\rm all}$ consists of all other trajectories in the sampled configuration, and $\|\cdot\|$ denotes spatial volume.
  - Explanation: Discarding excessive displacement and close endpoints leaves a supply of eligible labels. The deterministic replacement result applies to the entire eligible list.
- **claim-exact-unexposed-conditional-law** (construction, [source](#stmt-group-conditional-law)): For almost every exposed datum the normalizers in Equation <a href="#group:reference" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="group:reference">[group:reference]</a> are positive, and the conditional law of the unexposed trajectories is $$\label{group:product-law} \lambda_{\mathcal I}(\,\mathrm d\omega_{\mathcal I}) =\frac{1}{Z_{\mathcal I}} \mathbf 1_{\{\text{all pairs in $\mathcal I$ avoid each other}\}} \prod_{i\in\mathcal I}P_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i), \qquad Z_{\mathcal I}>0.$$ All reference trajectories obey their own regularity and confinement cutoffs. Each label has at most $C M$ other labels in this group with which a collision can be possible, where $C$ is a fixed geometric constant.
  - Explanation: Disintegration retains individual cutoffs and frozen-path avoidance in each reference law, then conditions the product on mutual avoidance. The resulting trajectories are coupled.
- **claim-unconditional-good-cell-supply** (bound, [source](#stmt-group-supply)): For any prescribed $p>0$, the constants before $M$ can be chosen as above, and then $M$ can be chosen sufficiently large, so that for sufficiently large $D$ and $K$, $$\label{group:bad-bound} \mathbb P_{\mathcal B}\{A\subset\{\text{bad sites}\}\}\le p^{\|A\|}$$ for every set $A$ of distinct sites. The bound is uniform in the group index, the ground function, and the allowed $\alpha$-window. It remains valid if $M$ is increased before increasing the lower threshold on $D$.
  - Explanation: Certificates supply a high proportion of stronger labels. Independent site allocations and sampling without replacement yield the unconditional joint bad-cell bound.
- **claim-one-path-density-and-compatibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-group-comparison)): There is a constant $G\ge2$, fixed after $t$ and independent of $D,K,M$, such that $$\label{group:density-bound} \Pi_i^U\ll P_i,\qquad 0\le\frac{\,\mathrm d\Pi_i^U}{\,\mathrm dP_i}\le G, \qquad \int\frac{\,\mathrm d\Pi_i^U}{\,\mathrm dP_i}\,\mathrm dP_i=1.$$ There is also $\eta_D\to0$, uniform in the exposed datum, with the following property. Fix any regular, confined trajectory of a distinct label in $\mathcal I$, keeping its recorded endpoints. Under either $P_i$ or $\Pi_i^U$, the probability of colliding with that deterministic trajectory is at most $\eta_D$. Its label need not be accessible. One may take $$\label{group:eta} \eta_D=C_t\bigl(tr+rD^{3\gamma}h_0^{-1/2}\bigr) \le C_t' D^{-1/4}.$$
  - Explanation: A midpoint Gaussian lower bound controls proposal density relative to the reference. The single-obstacle estimate survives conditioning and remains uniform over deterministic permitted opponents.
- **claim-good-routes-annealed-encounters** (construction, [source](#stmt-lat-paths)): Fix an integer $R\ge1$. There are constants $p_0,\kappa>0$, $C_{\rm path}<\infty$, and an integer $K_0$, depending only on $R$, with the following property. Suppose $K\ge K_0$ and <a href="#lat:bad-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lat:bad-law">[lat:bad-law]</a> holds with $p\le p_0$. For every admissible ordered pair $(v,w)$ there is a $\mathcal D$-measurable event $\mathcal E_{v,w}$ of probability at least $1/2$. On this event there is a measurable probability kernel on simple good nearest-neighbor paths from $v$ to $w$. Two independent draws from this kernel, conditional on $\mathcal D$, satisfy $$\label{lat:encounter} \mathbb E\left[ \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_{v,w}} \mathbb E\left[e^{\kappa J_R(\pi,\pi')}\mid\mathcal D\right] \right]\le C_{\rm path}.$$ The constants are uniform in the datum, its law, and the endpoint pair. For every sufficiently large $K$, at least $\upsilon_0K^6$ ordered pairs are admissible.
  - Explanation: A random skeleton is rerouted around finite bad components. Joint obstacle probabilities and both imported moments control encounters after averaging a common environment.
- **claim-connected-good-exterior-shell** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lat-boundary)): The set $\operatorname{fill}(C)$ lies in the coordinate bounding box of $C$. The exterior vertices at maximum distance one from $C$ form a nearest-neighbor connected set, denoted $\partial_*^{\rm ext}C$, with at most $26\|C\|$ vertices. If $C$ is a $*$-component of a larger bad set, every vertex of this shell is good.
  - Explanation: Filling stays within the coordinate box, and the exterior shell is a connected corridor of good vertices when the cluster is a bad component.
- **claim-small-periodic-component-lifts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lat-small-lifts)): Suppose $K>2a$, and a range-$a$ component on $\mathcal L_K$ has $m$ vertices with $am<K$. Each of its lifts to $\mathbb Z^3$ is a finite component projecting bijectively onto it. The coordinate span of a lift is at most $a(m-1)$.
  - Explanation: A spanning-tree lift and the strict size condition exclude winding, producing finite bijective lifts with bounded coordinate span.
- **claim-common-measure-affinity-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-holder)): Let $P,Q$ be finite nonnegative measures and let $\nu$ be a finite measure of mass $m>0$, absolutely continuous with respect to both. If $$\int\left(\frac{\,\mathrm d\nu}{\,\mathrm dP}\right)^2\,\mathrm dP\le C_P, \qquad \int\left(\frac{\,\mathrm d\nu}{\,\mathrm dQ}\right)^2\,\mathrm dQ\le C_Q,$$ then $\mathsf H(P,Q)\ge m^2/\sqrt{C_PC_Q}$.
  - Explanation: A common positive-mass measure with bounded squared likelihoods forces positive affinity through Hölder's inequality.
- **claim-disjoint-tag-group-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-tag-normalization)): For each ordered pair of cells, $$\label{cmp:group-affinity} \mathsf H(P_{vw},Q_{vw}) \ge\frac1D\sum_{g=1}^{G_D}\mathsf H(\widehat P_g,\widehat Q_g).$$
  - Explanation: Disjoint tag groups contribute submeasures of the full tagged position law. Projection and affinity superadditivity give the density normalization.
- **claim-conditional-normalizer-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-cancellation)): Consider two changes $S,T$ of the preceding kind, with $G\ge1$, and put $U=S\cup T$. Suppose that the following conditions hold.* 1. *There is $\chi\in[0,1)$ such that inserting any coordinate of $U$ from its reference $P_i$, or any factor of either proposal from its $\Pi_{S,i}$ or $\Pi_{T,i}$, satisfies all checks incident to that insertion with probability at least $1-\chi$. This bound is uniform in every fixed choice of the other individually permitted coordinates. It remains valid when any checks or coordinates have been deleted.* 2. *There are decompositions $S=C_S\sqcup F_S$ and $T=C_T\sqcup F_T$, with $F_S\cap T=F_T\cap S=\varnothing$. No coordinate carried by an $F_S$ factor has a check with any coordinate carried by a $T$ factor, or with the original primary coordinates indexed by $T$; the corresponding assertion holds with $S,T$ interchanged. The reference coordinates in $F_S,F_T$ also have no mutual checks.* *Then $$\label{cmp:cancellation-bound} \int L_SL_T\,\mathrm d\lambda \le G^{\|S\cap T\|}(1-\chi)^{-2(\|C_S\|+\|C_T\|)}.$$ It is enough that the assumptions hold for almost every fixed admissible configuration outside $U$.
  - Explanation: Uniform insertion compatibility controls near normalizers. Noninteraction of far factors makes their reference and proposal normalizers cancel exactly in the likelihood product.
- **claim-uniform-modulus-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-modulus)): After the fixed choices above, there are constants $D_0<\infty$ and $$\label{cmp:c-star} c_*:=\frac{\upsilon_0\ell}{32M G C_{\mathrm{path}}}>0, \qquad \upsilon_0=\frac1{2000}$$ such that, whenever $D\ge D_0$, $\alpha=Dr\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$, and $K$ is sufficiently large, every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function satisfies $B(\Phi)\ge c_*$. The constant $c_*$ is unchanged if the lower threshold on $D$ is subsequently increased. The volume thresholds can be taken locally uniform for $D$ in compact subintervals of $[D_0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: The common measure gives a fixed affinity gain for each group and admissible cell pair. Summing groups and pairs produces a positive modulus fraction that survives a higher dilution threshold.
- **claim-retained-slice-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-connectivity)): For every bath $Y$, any two points of $G(Y)$ can be joined by a continuous path in $S_Y$ whose distance from the forbidden set is strictly positive.
  - Explanation: Exterior-shell detours through good microcubes connect retained points with positive clearance from the closed forbidden balls.
- **claim-small-fill-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-small-fills)): For $K>10$ and $r\le1$, the projected union of small fills has volume at most $C r^2 N$, with an absolute constant $C$.
  - Explanation: Each occupied column of a small fill can be charged to a bad cube, bounding its total volume by cross-sectional area times the number of obstacle cubes.
- **claim-large-component-close-witnesses** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-large-witnesses)): There are absolute constants $r_1,c_{\rm g}>0$ such that the following holds for $r\le r_1$ and $K>10$. If a unit cell $B_v$ contains a microcube from a large bad component, then at least $c_{\rm g}/r$ distinct bath particles lie within distance $5$ of $v$, and each has a distinct bath particle within distance $6r$. One may take $c_{\rm g}=1/(8C_0)$ with $C_0=343$.
  - Explanation: A simple obstacle-cube path supplies many distinct bath labels. The connected graph of successive distinct witnesses gives each label a close partner.
- **claim-bath-averaged-omitted-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-omitted-volume)): Fix the constants as above and take $D$ sufficiently large. There are absolute constants $C,C_{\rm an}$ such that, for $K\ge100$ and $C_{\rm an}p_D\le1/2$, $$\label{geom:volume-bound} \delta_{\rm geom}:=\int\frac{\|\mathcal A(Y)\|}{V}\,\nu(\,\mathrm dY) \le C r^2D+C p_D +K^3(C_{\rm an}p_D)^{\lfloor K/10\rfloor}.$$ The retained points belong to one component of the open slice $S_Y$ whenever the retained set is nonempty. The estimate is uniform over the normalized ground vector and $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$.
  - Explanation: Small fills have a deterministic volume cost. Large fills are bounded by close-count lattice animals and a separate periodic-component error.
- **claim-scaled-complex-ground-occupation** (bound, [source](#stmt-main-scaled)): There are absolute constants $\alpha_0,D_*,c_*>0$ such that, for $D=N/K^3\ge D_*$ and $r=\alpha/D$ with $\alpha\in[\alpha_0/2,2\alpha_0]$, every normalized complex bosonic ground vector satisfies $$B(\Psi)\ge c_*/2$$ for all sufficiently large integer $K$. The volume threshold can be chosen uniformly when $D$ ranges over a fixed compact subinterval of $[D_*,\infty)$, and is independent of the chosen ground vector.
  - Explanation: Fixing the modulus gain before increasing dilution makes the geometric phase loss small enough to retain a positive fraction for arbitrary complex ground vectors.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-main-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\varepsilon_0,c_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $a,\\rho>0$ satisfying $\\rho a^3<\\varepsilon_0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-setup-ground",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $n\\ge1$ with nonempty $\\Omega_n$, the full and bosonic Dirichlet operators have compact resolvent and the same bottom energy $E_n$. In particular, both $\\Psi$ and $\\Phi=|\\Psi|$ are full ground eigenfunctions at energy $E_N$. They have smooth representatives in $\\Omega_N$. On every connected component $C$ of $\\Omega_N$, either both vanish identically or $$\\Phi>0,\\qquad \\Psi=e^{i\\vartheta_C}\\Phi$$ for one constant $\\vartheta_C\\in\\mathbb R$. For every real bounded Lipschitz function $u$ on $\\Lambda_K^N$, $$\\label{setup:multiplier}\n q_N[u\\Phi]-E_N\\lVert u\\Phi\\rVert_2^2\n                 =\\int\\Phi^2|\\nabla u|^2.$$ The gradient on the right includes all particle coordinates.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-setup-phase-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\{(x,Y):x\\in U_Y\\}$ be measurable. Suppose that for almost every bath with $m(Y)>0$, the set $U_Y$ is contained in one connected component of $S_Y$, or is empty.         "anchor": "stmt-sp-cube",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a Euclidean cube of side $l$, and let $F\\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever $|x_i-x_j|\\le r$ for some $i\\ne j$. With no boundary condition on the outer faces, $$\\label{sp:cube-bound}\n \\sum_{i=1}^n\\int_{Q^n}|\\nabla_iF|^2\n \\ge c_{\\rm cube}\\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\\int_{Q^n}|F|^2,\n \\qquad c_{\\rm cube}=\\frac1{64\\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for every torus form function $F\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$ and every fixed label subset $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}$, $$\\label{sp:cell-energy}\n q_I[F]\\ge c_{\\rm cube}r\\int\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal L}\n                    n_v^I(n_v^I-1)|F|^2.$$ For $D\\ge2$ this implies $$\\label{sp:energy-lower}\n \\frac{E_N}{N}\\ge c_E\\alpha,\n \\qquad c_E=c_{\\rm cube}/2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a Euclidean cube of side $l$, and let $F\\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever $|x_i-x_j|\\le r$ for some $i\\ne j$.         "exact_quote": "Let $0<r<s<1$, let $F\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$, and fix a label set $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}$.         "anchor": "stmt-sp-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w$ be a real normalized smooth one-particle orbital and $F$ an $n$-particle form function.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-path-slab",
        "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#path:law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"path:law\">[path:law]</a> defines a probability measure. Every time marginal is $\\mu$. Its restriction to an interval of length $s$ has the same construction with endpoint multiplier $e^{sE_N}\\Phi(X_-)\\Phi(X_+)$, and it is invariant under time reversal. Given both endpoint configurations, it is the product torus-bridge law conditioned on mutual hard avoidance. If the winding increments are also fixed, the underlying product consists of the corresponding Euclidean bridges.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-path-slab",
          "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#path:law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"path:law\">[path:law]</a> defines a probability measure. Every time marginal is $\\mu$. Its restriction to an interval of length $s$ has the same construction with endpoint multiplier $e^{sE_N}\\Phi(X_-)\\Phi(X_+)$, and it is invariant under time reversal. Given both endpoint configurations, it is the product torus-bridge law conditioned on mutual hard avoidance. If the winding increments are also fixed, the underlying product consists of the corresponding Euclidean bridges.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "path:law",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu_N$ be the measure on $N$ independent torus Brownian motions of generator $\\Delta$, on $[0,2t]$, with Lebesgue initial measure. Write $\\omega=(\\omega_1,\\ldots,\\omega_N)$ and $X(u)=(\\omega_1(u),\\ldots,\\omega_N(u))$. Set $Y=X(0)$ and $Z=X(2t)$. Define $$\\label{path:law}\n \\,\\mathrm d\\mathcal R(\\omega)=e^{2tE_N}\\Phi(Y)\\Phi(Z)\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{d(\\omega_i(u),\\omega_j(u))>r\\ \\forall u,\\ i<j\\}}\n \\,\\mathrm d\\nu_N(\\omega).$$ Here and below $d$ is the torus distance. A continuous path has a continuous Euclidean lift after its initial representative is chosen; its lifted increments and diameter do not depend on that representative.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Equation <a href=\"#path:law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"path:law\">[path:law]</a> defines a probability measure. Every time marginal is $\\mu$. Given both endpoint configurations, it is the product torus-bridge law conditioned on mutual hard avoidance. If the winding increments are also fixed, the underlying product consists of the corresponding Euclidean bridges.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-path-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $q>0$ and fixed $t>0$, a free Brownian path fails regularity with probability at most $C_{q,t}D^{-q}$. The bound is uniform in its initial point, $K$, and the allowed $\\alpha$-window. It also holds for a Euclidean bridge of duration $2t$ with bounded endpoint displacement, or for two independent Euclidean bridge legs of duration $t$ joined at a prescribed middle point, when the endpoints and middle point lie in a fixed bounded region. The bound and the required lower threshold on $D$ may depend on that region and on $t$; the numerical regularity threshold $C_{\\rm reg}$ is fixed once for all these laws.*\n\n*If all three prescribed points lie within distance $3$ of a fixed site $v$, the probability that the two-leg path leaves the ball of radius $10$ about $v$ is at most $Ce^{-c/t}$, with numerical $c,C>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.5"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $y_1,\\ldots,y_m\\in\\mathbb R^3$ have pairwise distances at least $s_0$, where $m\\ge\\ell D/2$ and $s_0=bD^{-1/3}$. Fix $u\\in\\mathbb R^3$ and $0<s\\le t/2$.         "exact_quote": "There is a constant $G\\ge2$, fixed after $t$ and independent of $D,K,M$, such that $$\\label{group:density-bound}\n \\Pi_i^U\\ll P_i,\\qquad\n 0\\le\\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\Pi_i^U}{\\,\\mathrm dP_i}\\le G,\n \\qquad \\int\\frac{\\,\\mathrm d\\Pi_i^U}{\\,\\mathrm dP_i}\\,\\mathrm dP_i=1.$$ There is also $\\eta_D\\to0$, uniform in the exposed datum, with the following property.         "anchor": "stmt-cmp-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two changes $S,T$ of the preceding kind, with $G\\ge1$, and put $U=S\\cup T$. Suppose that the following conditions hold.*\n\n1.  *There is $\\chi\\in[0,1)$ such that inserting any coordinate of $U$ from its reference $P_i$, or any factor of either proposal from its $\\Pi_{S,i}$ or $\\Pi_{T,i}$, satisfies all checks incident to that insertion with probability at least $1-\\chi$. This bound is uniform in every fixed choice of the other individually permitted coordinates. It remains valid when any checks or coordinates have been deleted.*\n\n2.  *There are decompositions $S=C_S\\sqcup F_S$ and $T=C_T\\sqcup F_T$, with $F_S\\cap T=F_T\\cap S=\\varnothing$. No coordinate carried by an $F_S$ factor has a check with any coordinate carried by a $T$ factor, or with the original primary coordinates indexed by $T$; the corresponding assertion holds with $S,T$ interchanged. The reference coordinates in $F_S,F_T$ also have no mutual checks.*\n\n\n*Then $$\\label{cmp:cancellation-bound}\n \\int L_SL_T\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda\n \\le G^{|S\\cap T|}(1-\\chi)^{-2(|C_S|+|C_T|)}.$$ It is enough that the assumptions hold for almost every fixed admissible configuration outside $U$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cmp-modulus",
        "exact_quote": "After the fixed choices above, there are constants $D_0<\\infty$ and $$\\label{cmp:c-star}\n c_*:=\\frac{\\upsilon_0\\ell}{32M G C_{\\mathrm{path}}}>0,\n \\qquad \\upsilon_0=\\frac1{2000}$$ such that, whenever $D\\ge D_0$, $\\alpha=Dr\\in[\\alpha_0/2,2\\alpha_0]$, and $K$ is sufficiently large, every normalized nonnegative bosonic ground function satisfies $B(\\Phi)\\ge c_*$.         "source_id": "PM-267.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hard-sphere configuration set and Hamiltonian** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $N$ particles and exclusion distance $a>0$, define the open configuration set $$\Omega_{N,L,a} =\{X\in\Lambda_L^N:d_L(x_i,x_j)>a\text{ whenever }i<j\}.$$ The hard-sphere Hamiltonian is the operator associated with the Dirichlet form $$q_{N,L,a}[f]=\sum_{i=1}^N\int_{\Omega_{N,L,a}}\|\nabla_i f\|^2, \qquad f\in H_0^1(\Omega_{N,L,a}),$$ restricted to symmetric functions.
- **Constant-orbital occupation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We measure condensation by $$\label{main:occupation} B(\Psi)=\frac{\langle\varphi_{0,L}, \Gamma^{(1)}_\Psi\varphi_{0,L}\rangle}{N} =\frac1{L^3}\int\left\|\int\Psi(x,Y)\,\,\mathrm dx\right\|^2\,\mathrm dY.$$
- **Scaled labelled-particle form** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The full, labelled-particle Dirichlet form, without a symmetry restriction, is $$q_n[f]=\sum_{i=1}^n\int_{\Omega_n}\|\nabla_i f\|^2, \qquad D(q_n)=H_0^1(\Omega_n).$$
- **Scaled density and exclusion product** ([source](#setup:scales)): The mean particle count per unit cell and its product with the exclusion distance are $$\label{setup:scales} D=\frac{N}{K^3},\qquad \alpha=Dr, \qquad \frac{\alpha_0}{2}\le\alpha\le2\alpha_0, \qquad D\ge D_0.$$
- **Invariant gas parameter** ([source](#setup:gas-parameter)): The gas parameter is unchanged: $$\label{setup:gas-parameter} Dr^3=\frac{N}{L^3}a^3=\frac{\alpha^3}{D^2}.$$
- **Position law and bath marginal** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For the rest of the proof put $$\label{setup:position-law} \Phi=\|\Psi\|,\qquad \mu(\,\mathrm dX)=\Phi(X)^2\,\,\mathrm dX, \qquad m(Y)=\int\Phi(x,Y)^2\,\,\mathrm dx.$$
- **Weighted spatial count and close count** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For nonempty $A\subset\mathcal L$, use its site centers as a subset of the torus and define $$\begin{aligned} f_A(X)&=\sum_{i=1}^N e^{-d(x_i,A)},\label{sp:weighted-count}\\ k_A(X)&=\#\{i:d(x_i,A)\le6,\ d_i\le2s_0\}, \qquad d_i=\min_{j\ne i}d(x_i,x_j),\label{sp:localized-close-count}\\ s_0&=bD^{-1/3},\qquad 0<b<1.\label{sp:separation-scale} \end{aligned}$$
- **Endpoint-weighted stationary path law** ([source](#path:law)): Define $$\label{path:law} \,\mathrm d\mathcal R(\omega)=e^{2tE_N}\Phi(Y)\Phi(Z) \mathbf 1_{\{d(\omega_i(u),\omega_j(u))>r\ \forall u,\ i<j\}} \,\mathrm d\nu_N(\omega).$$
- **Regular path** ([source](#stmt-path-regular-path)): Fix a sufficiently large numerical constant $C_{\rm reg}$. A path is *regular* if $$\label{path:regular-conditions} \max_j A_j\le D^\gamma, \qquad \frac1{2m_t}\sum_{j=1}^{2m_t}(1+A_j)^3\le C_{\rm reg},$$ and its lifted diameter on each of $[0,h_0]$ and $[2t-h_0,2t]$ is at most $s_0/8$.
- **Eligible label** ([source](#pm-h-32)): A label $i$ is *eligible at $v$* if 1. $Y_i\in B_v$ and $d(Z_i,v)\le3$; 2. $d(Y_i,Y_j)>s_0$ and $d(Z_i,Z_j)>s_0$ for every $j\ne i$; 3. its trajectory is regular and remains in the radius-$10$ ball about $v$.
- **Group reference path law** ([source](#group:reference)): Define $$\label{group:reference} P_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i)= \frac{\mathbf 1_{C_i(F)}\,B_i(\,\mathrm d\omega_i)}{a_i}, \qquad a_i=B_i(C_i(F)).$$
- **Accessible label and good site** ([source](#group:targets)): A label is *accessible* if $\|T_i(w)\|\ge0.99$ for all seven target cells. This property and all the sets $T_i(w)$ depend only on $\mathcal B$. Let $\mathcal A_v$ be the accessible labels of the fixed group at $v$. A site is *good* if $\|\mathcal A_v\|\ge M/2$, and *bad* otherwise.
- **Lattice path encounter count** ([source](#lat:contact-definition)): For two simple paths $\pi,\pi'$ define $$\label{lat:contact-definition} J_R(\pi,\pi')= \sum_{z\in\pi}\sum_{z'\in\pi'} \mathbf 1_{\{d_\infty(z,z')\le R\}}.$$
- **Discrete fill** ([source](#pm-h-38)): For a finite $*$-connected nonempty set $C\subset\mathbb Z^3$, let $\operatorname{fill}(C)$ consist of $C$ and all finite nearest-neighbor components of $\mathbb Z^3\setminus C$.
- **Measure affinity** ([source](#cmp:affinity-superadditive)): For finite nonnegative measures $P,Q$ on the same measurable space, define their affinity by $$\mathsf H(P,Q)=\int\sqrt{pq}\,\mathrm d\xi, \qquad p=\frac{\,\mathrm dP}{\,\mathrm d\xi},\quad q=\frac{\,\mathrm dQ}{\,\mathrm d\xi}, \quad \xi=P+Q.$$
- **Cell-pair position measures** ([source](#pm-h-43)): For ordered cells $v,w$, define finite measures on $(x,y,Y)\in B_v\times B_w\times\Lambda_K^{N-1}$ by $$\label{cmp:cell-measures} \,\mathrm dP_{vw}=V\Phi(x,Y)^2\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dy\,\mathrm dY, \qquad \,\mathrm dQ_{vw}=V\Phi(y,Y)^2\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dy\,\mathrm dY.$$
- **Individually permitted configuration** ([source](#pm-h-45)): A configuration is *individually permitted* when each coordinate lies in its allowed set; this imposes no pairwise checks.
- **Retained bath region** ([source](#pm-h-49)): Define $\mathcal A(Y)\subset\Lambda_K$ as the projection of all closed cubes with indices in the fills of the bad components. If the lifted bad set has an infinite component, define $\mathcal A(Y)=\Lambda_K$. Use this convention also for inadmissible baths. The retained region is $$G(Y)=\Lambda_K\setminus\mathcal A(Y).$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-thermodynamic-occupation-floor | \liminf_{k\to\infty} B(\Psi_k) | ≥ | $c_0$ | Fixed positive physical density and exclusion distance; arbitrary thermodynamic sequences. |
| q-gas-parameter-invariance | Dr^3 | = | $\frac{\alpha^3}{D^2}$ | Scaled density and exclusion distance correspond to the original particle density and scattering length. |
| q-phase-transfer-error | B(\Psi) | ≥ | $B(\Phi)-4\sqrt{\delta_{\rm geom}}$ | The discarded volume is averaged with the ground-state bath marginal. |
| q-cube-coercivity-constant | c_{\rm cube} | = | $\frac1{64\sqrt3}$ | The cube side and particle number enter the displayed kinetic bound. |
| q-ground-energy-lower-constant | c_E | = | $c_{\rm cube}/2$ | Scaled density in the stated energy regime. |
| q-close-neighbor-coercivity-constant | c_{\rm near} | = | $\frac{27}{262144\sqrt3}$ | The exclusion radius is below the close-neighbor scale. |
| q-insertion-volume-threshold | C\lVert w\rVert_\infty^2kr^3 | ≤ | $\frac14$ | The constant depends on the fixed radial cutoff profile. |
| q-removal-energy-gap | E_N-E_{N-m} | ≤ | $C\alpha m$ | Uniform in the prescribed exclusion-density product window. |
| q-joint-low-count-tail | \mu\{n_v<\ell D\text{ for every }v\in A\} | ≤ | $(C/D^2)^{\|A\|}$ | The constants and the lower-count threshold are independent of volume and ground vector. |
| q-close-count-site-tail-base | p_D | = | $\left(C_{\theta}b^{-2}D^{-4/3}\right)^{1/Q}$ | Fix the close-count threshold, then the separation parameter, before increasing scaled density. |
| q-regularity-mesh-exponent | \gamma | = | $\frac1{12}$ | The numerical exponent is fixed before increasing density. |
| q-averaged-bridge-collision | \frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m Q_{y_i,z_i}^{B}(\mathcal C_v\cap\mathcal H(c)) | ≤ | $Ctr+C\frac{rD^{3\gamma}}{D\sqrt{h_0}}$ | Separated endpoint lists; the constant is independent of small time. |
| q-single-obstacle-collision-decay | \eta_D | ≤ | $C_t' D^{-1/4}$ | Time is fixed; the bound is uniform in exposed data and independent of later group size. |
| q-disjoint-group-count | G_D | = | $\left\lfloor\frac{\ell D}{4M}\right\rfloor$ | Group size is fixed before the final density threshold. |
| q-accepted-route-event-mass | \mathbb P(\mathcal E_{v,w}) | ≥ | $1/2$ | The event is determined by the datum before route draws. |
| q-exterior-shell-size | \left\|\partial_*^{\rm ext}C\right\| | ≤ | $26\|C\|$ | Exterior neighbors visible from the infinite nearest-neighbor complement. |
| q-normalizer-cancellation-bound | \int L_SL_T\,\mathrm d\lambda | ≤ | $G^{\|S\cap T\|}(1-\chi)^{-2(\|C_S\|+\|C_T\|)}$ | Uniform insertion compatibility, bounded primary marginal densities, and separated far factors. |
| q-modulus-occupation-fraction | c_* | = | $\frac{\upsilon_0\ell}{32M G C_{\mathrm{path}}}$ | The fraction is fixed before any later increase of the dilution threshold. |
| q-small-fill-volume-cost | projected union of small fills | ≤ | $C r^2 N$ | Small components have bounded physical coordinate span. |
| q-large-component-witness-coefficient | c_{\rm g} | = | $1/(8C_0)$ | The microcube intersection constant is the stated absolute constant. |
| q-omitted-volume-bound | \delta_{\rm geom} | ≤ | $C r^2D+C p_D +K^3(C_{\rm an}p_D)^{\lfloor K/10\rfloor}$ | Dilution reduces local errors before the fixed-density volume limit removes the periodic-component error. |
| q-scaled-complex-occupation-floor | B(\Psi) | ≥ | $c_*/2$ | Volume thresholds are locally uniform in scaled density and independent of the ground vector. |

With no boundary condition on the outer faces, $$\\label{sp:cube-bound}\n \\sum_{i=1}^n\\int_{Q^n}|\\nabla_iF|^2\n \\ge c_{\\rm cube}\\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\\int_{Q^n}|F|^2,\n \\qquad c_{\\rm cube}=\\frac1{64\\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for every torus form function $F\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$ and every fixed label subset $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}$, $$\\label{sp:cell-energy}\n q_I[F]\\ge c_{\\rm cube}r\\int\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal L}\n                    n_v^I(n_v^I-1)|F|^2.$$ For $D\\ge2$ this implies $$\\label{sp:energy-lower}\n \\frac{E_N}{N}\\ge c_E\\alpha,\n \\qquad c_E=c_{\\rm cube}/2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        }

The reference coordinates in $F_S,F_T$ also have no mutual checks.*\n\n\n*Then $$\\label{cmp:cancellation-bound}\n \\int L_SL_T\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda\n \\le G^{|S\\cap T|}(1-\\chi)^{-2(|C_S|+|C_T|)}.$$ It is enough that the assumptions hold for almost every fixed admissible configuration outside $U$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-ground-form-and-phases** [claim](#stmt-setup-ground) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-ground): Compact form embedding, root-mean-square symmetrization, modulus contraction, elliptic regularity, and the equality case of the kinetic identity. -> Full ground equation, componentwise phases, and multiplier identity.
- **step-allowed-coordinate-slices** [claim](#stmt-setup-slices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-slices): Compactly supported approximation and Fubini convergence in the unfixed coordinates. -> Allowed Sobolev slices and common phase on connected subsets.
- **step-quantify-phase-loss** [claim](#stmt-setup-phase-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-phase-transfer): Compare the occupation integrands on the retained set and apply Cauchy–Schwarz to its complement. -> Transfer inequality from modulus occupation to complex occupation.
- **step-cube-energy** [claim](#stmt-sp-cube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-cube): Integrate along rays from a hard-core partner, subdivide the cube, and sum assignment sectors. -> Cube coercivity, unit-cell energy penalty, and ground-energy lower bound.
- **step-close-neighbor-energy** [claim](#stmt-sp-close-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-close-energy): Average partner-based ray coercivity over translated grids. -> Kinetic penalty for close-neighbor labels.
- **step-configurationwise-insertion** [claim](#stmt-sp-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-insertion): Cut off a smooth orbital near old particles and normalize it at each old configuration. -> Admissible added-particle trial preserving the old marginal and additive energy cost.
- **step-removal-gaps** [claim](#stmt-sp-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-removal): Repeated constant-orbital insertion and averaging kinetic energies over label subsets. -> Removal-gap upper bound and energy-per-particle monotonicity.
- **step-joint-low-counts** [claim](#stmt-sp-low-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-low-counts): Insert particles into deficient cells, derive an energy penalty, and induct over products of count cutoffs. -> Joint low-count tail for arbitrary cell sets.
- **step-weighted-localization** [claim](#stmt-sp-localized-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-localized-form): Use a randomized-radius product partition, retain outside ground energy, and complete a scalar square in the local count penalty. -> Weighted count and close-neighbor form penalty.
- **step-joint-spatial-tails** [claim](#stmt-sp-joint-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-joint-tails): Apply the multiplier identity to exponential weights and separated close-count cutoff products, then use lattice coloring. -> Spatial count moments and joint close-count tails.
- **step-stationary-killed-law** [claim](#stmt-path-slab) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-slab): Exhaust the open allowed set and use the ground-state semigroup equation in the killed heat kernel. -> Normalized stationary reversible path law and endpoint-conditional bridge law.
- **step-selected-interaction-deletion** [claim](#stmt-path-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-deletion): Drop selected collision restrictions, bound the remaining semigroup, and reverse selected free paths. -> Free-path comparison for selected nonnegative reversal-invariant functionals.
- **step-free-regularity** [claim](#stmt-path-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-regularity): Gaussian mesh tails, centered high moments, and Brownian representations of bridges. -> Uniform rare failure bounds and confinement estimate.
- **step-free-visit-moments** [claim](#stmt-path-free-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-free-visits): Count unit-ball exits and combine exponential moments, travel tails, and the rare-event probability by Hölder. -> Spatially decaying free visit moment.
- **step-interacting-visit-moments** [claim](#stmt-path-visit-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-visit-moments): Expand over selected label subsets, apply path deletion, and close with the spatial count moment. -> Interacting visit exponential moment.
- **step-joint-certificates** [claim](#stmt-path-certificates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-path-certificates): Bound each spatial and path failure type jointly and sum over type assignments. -> Unconditional joint certificate-failure estimate.
- **step-gaussian-endpoint-packing** [claim](#stmt-bridge-gaussian-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bridge-gaussian-packing): Pack disjoint balls around separated Gaussian means and sum Gaussian annuli. -> Averaged bridge density bound.
- **step-averaged-bridge-collisions** [claim](#stmt-bridge-averaged-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bridge-averaged-hit): Exclude outer windows, estimate early intervals with endpoint packing and late intervals by midpoint averaging, then sum the mesh. -> Averaged collision estimate with a time-independent coefficient.
- **step-single-bridge-collision** [claim](#stmt-bridge-single-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bridge-single-hit): Replace averaged endpoint densities by single Gaussian densities and apply forward and reversed bridge orientations. -> Single replacement collision probability tending to zero.
- **step-successful-midpoint-sets** [claim](#stmt-bridge-success-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bridge-success-supply): Union-bound regular nearby obstacles and use averaging thresholds for labels and target cells. -> Many labels with large successful target sets.
- **step-certified-eligible-labels** [claim](#stmt-group-certified-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-group-certified-labels): Remove long-moving and close-endpoint labels, then apply successful target supply to the entire eligible list. -> Eligible-label lower bound and stronger success property.
- **step-exact-group-disintegration** [claim](#stmt-group-conditional-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-group-conditional-law): Condition on endpoints and eligibility, expose outside trajectories, and absorb individual cutoffs into normalized references. -> Exact mutual-avoidance conditioned product with positive normalizers.
- **step-unconditional-good-groups** [claim](#stmt-group-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-group-supply): Sample without replacement at certified sites and combine conditional allocation failures with unconditional certificate failures. -> Joint bad-site bound for every fixed group.
- **step-one-path-reference-comparison** [claim](#stmt-group-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-group-comparison): Use the bridge midpoint Gaussian lower bound and divide single-obstacle probabilities by uniformly positive success masses. -> Bounded proposal likelihood and uniform deterministic compatibility.
- **step-exterior-shell-corridor** [claim](#stmt-lat-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lat-boundary): Use coordinate rays for fill containment and exterior boundary connectivity for a good nearest-neighbor shell. -> Connected good exterior shell and its size bound.
- **step-periodic-component-lifting** [claim](#stmt-lat-small-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lat-small-lifts): Lift a spanning tree and show every remaining short edge has zero period displacement. -> Finite bijective lifts and bounded span.
- **step-good-path-encounter-law** [claim](#stmt-lat-paths) / [proof](#lat:moment-reduction): Reject large components and filled endpoints, reroute independent skeletons along exterior shells, count bad animals, and apply both imported moments. -> Measurable good-route kernel with an annealed encounter bound and many admissible pairs.
- **step-common-measure-holder** [claim](#stmt-cmp-holder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-holder): Apply Hölder to densities of a common finite measure and the target measures. -> Affinity lower bound.
- **step-tag-group-affinity** [claim](#stmt-cmp-tag-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-tag-normalization): Project separate data spaces, sum disjoint tag contributions, and apply affinity monotonicity and superadditivity. -> Group affinity contribution with the correct density normalization.
- **step-near-only-normalizer-cost** [claim](#stmt-cmp-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-cancellation): Disintegrate auxiliary kernels, delete near factors according to the stated convention, and cancel far reference and proposal normalizers. -> Likelihood-product bound charged only to overlap and near factors.
- **step-modulus-occupation** [claim](#stmt-cmp-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-modulus): Construct common midpoint assignments, average route and label likelihoods, apply cancellation and the encounter moment, then sum affinity gains. -> Uniform positive modulus occupation.
- **step-retained-slice-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-geom-connectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-connectivity): Remove bad visits from a finite microcube route by exterior-shell detours, then join centers through closed good cubes. -> A common allowed slice component with strict clearance.
- **step-small-fill-volume** [claim](#stmt-geom-small-fills) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-small-fills): Charge filled columns to bad cubes and sum the bounded number of cubes meeting each bath ball. -> Deterministic small-fill volume bound.
- **step-large-fill-particle-witnesses** [claim](#stmt-geom-large-witnesses) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-large-witnesses): Follow a simple obstacle path, count distinct witnessing balls, and use the connected graph of successive labels. -> Many distinct nearby bath particles each with a distinct close partner.
- **step-omitted-bath-volume** [claim](#stmt-geom-omitted-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-omitted-volume): Integrate under the full position law, bound coarse bad animals, lift small periodic components, and sum affected-cell probabilities. -> Small expected omitted volume with a separate finite-volume error.
- **step-scaled-complex-bound** [claim](#stmt-main-scaled) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-scaled): Fix all modulus parameters, increase dilution to control discarded volume, and apply phase transfer. -> Complex ground-state occupation lower bound with locally uniform volume thresholds.
- **step-fixed-physical-thermodynamic-limit** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-scaled-2): Choose an integer scaled side keeping the exclusion-density product near its fixed value, then use unitary rescaling and local uniformity. -> Uniform positive limiting occupation at fixed physical density and exclusion distance.
- **step-ground-space-convexity** [claim](#stmt-main-mixed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-main-scaled-3): Decompose a ground-supported density operator into pure ground vectors and use linearity of constant-orbital occupation. -> The same bound for ground-space mixtures and normalized projections.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fixed-parameter-condensation**: The constant orbital has a positive lower limiting occupation uniformly over normalized complex bosonic ground vectors at sufficiently small fixed gas parameter.
- **invariant-componentwise-phase**: The modulus is a full ground eigenfunction at the same energy as the original vector. On each positive connected component, the original vector differs from its modulus by a constant phase.
- **invariant-connected-slice-phase-transfer**: A measurable retained region contained in a common allowed-slice component permits transfer of the modulus occupation bound to the complex vector, with loss controlled by the bath-weighted discarded volume.
- **invariant-exact-conditioned-product**: At almost every exposed datum, the unexposed trajectories follow their individually restricted product references conditioned on mutual hard avoidance, with positive normalizers.
- **invariant-annealed-encounter-control**: Independent route draws share a common environment, and their exponential encounter moment is controlled after averaging over the retained environment event.
- **invariant-normalizer-cancellation**: The product likelihood moment charges shared coordinates and near factors while the far reference and proposal normalizers cancel.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-complete-condensation**: Interpret the positive occupation fraction as complete condensation or an optimal depletion estimate. -> The established lower fraction does not provide the sharper dilute depletion behavior.
- **anti-use-thermal-density-operators**: Apply the mixed-state corollary to arbitrary thermal density operators. -> A density operator with support outside the bosonic ground eigenspace fails the corollary's support requirement.
- **anti-use-unrestricted-density**: Apply the occupation lower bound without checking dilution. -> The theorem does not cover a gas parameter outside its sufficiently small fixed regime.
- **anti-use-global-phase-assumption**: Replace the complex ground vector by its modulus and assume their occupations coincide globally. -> Distinct positive configuration components may carry different phases, so a modulus bound alone does not prove the complex-vector bound.
- **anti-use-prescribed-midpoint-collision-bound**: Use the averaged bridge collision estimate uniformly at a prescribed midpoint. -> Choosing the midpoint on the obstacle path forces a collision at the middle time.
- **anti-use-independent-unexposed-paths**: Treat the conditional unexposed trajectories as independent after imposing the individual cutoffs. -> Mutual hard avoidance couples the product references and contributes an interaction normalizer.
- **anti-use-fixed-environment-encounter-bound**: Read the route encounter estimate as a uniform bound for every retained fixed environment. -> The moment bound averages over the common environment and does not give the claimed pointwise guarantee.
- **anti-use-restoring-deleted-auxiliary-obstacles**: Replace a deleted auxiliary path instance by an original reference trajectory carrying the same particle label. -> This changes the reduced checks and normalizers, invalidating the far-factor cancellation argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The condensation conclusion is an analytical thermodynamic-limit statement over arbitrary ground eigenvectors, not a finite constructive computation. The source specifies: "There are absolute constants $\varepsilon_0,c_0>0$ with the following property." It requires diverging particle numbers and lengths at fixed density and exclusion distance. The proof uses continuous-path hard avoidance, conditional probability measures and existential parameter thresholds. Finite checks of lattice sublemmas would not decide this condensation conclusion or certify its uniformity over the ground space.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ground-state occupation theorem specifies an analytical property of wave functions and thermodynamic sequences, without supplying a finite procedure for computing ground eigenvectors or their occupation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stationary path representation involves continuous Brownian trajectories and avoidance throughout their duration. It does not specify a finite simulation whose sampled collision checks certify that law. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bridge comparison bounds require regular obstacles, separated endpoints and averaging of the midpoint over a target cell. They do not provide a general collision-testing or trajectory-planning tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The exterior-shell construction gives a finite deterministic procedure for repairing a supplied lattice walk around finite bad components. Each replacement removes a bad visit without introducing another, and chronological loop erasure yields a simple good nearest-neighbor path. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Finite periodic components can be represented by spanning-tree lifts and checked along additional edges. For a finite lift, filling is determined by connectivity inside a finite bounding box with the exterior attached, giving a concrete representation of discarded obstacle regions. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cube-energy coercivity estimate concerns Sobolev functions and kinetic energy integrals. It supplies no accelerator architecture, finite arithmetic kernel or resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The common-measure inequality bounds affinity through likelihood moments. Its measures and absolute-continuity assumptions provide no cryptographic construction or adversarial security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exact conditional trajectory law is a constrained product with interaction normalizers. The paper supplies neither a learning procedure nor a statistical estimation contract for recovering that law from data. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Normalized insertion constructs an admissible variational trial function under a local exclusion-volume condition. It does not solve a finite optimisation problem or certify an optimal particle arrangement. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional-normalizer cancellation requires uniform compatibility and explicit noninteraction of far factors. These measure-theoretic conditions do not constitute a distributed update protocol or a consistency guarantee. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal mathematical-physics result establishes a positive constant-orbital occupation in the dilute ground-state thermodynamic limit. It does not construct a finite method for determining the optimal depletion or the complete condensate fraction. |

By Lemma <a href=\"#lat:boundary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lat:boundary\">30</a>, join these two shell vertices by a simple nearest-neighbor path in the shell, replacing the intervening part of the walk. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-dilution-and-thermodynamic-order** (hypothesis): The physical exclusion distance and positive density are fixed before taking the thermodynamic limit, and the gas parameter must satisfy the stated dilution threshold. Blocked use: ['Applying the occupation bound outside the sufficiently dilute regime.', 'Replacing the fixed-parameter thermodynamic conclusion by an unrestricted joint limit.']
- **limit-periodic-bosonic-hard-core-model** (formal_scope): The Hamiltonian is the bosonic hard-core Dirichlet kinetic operator on the three-dimensional flat torus. The paper does not establish the same occupation theorem for trapped geometries, antisymmetric wave functions or general interaction potentials. Blocked use: ['Transferring the theorem directly to a trapped or nonperiodic gas.', 'Applying the result to fermionic states or replacing hard exclusion by a soft interaction without a new proof.']
- **limit-ground-supported-mixtures** (hypothesis): The mixed-state extension requires density operators supported on the bosonic ground eigenspaces under the main theorem's hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Invoking the corollary for thermal states or arbitrary mixtures containing excited-state contributions.']
- **limit-positive-fraction-only** (formal_scope): The conclusion supplies a positive occupation floor. It does not establish an optimal depletion law or convergence to complete condensation as dilution increases. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the lower bound as complete condensation.', 'Extracting an optimal depletion asymptotic from the positive occupation floor.']
- **limit-componentwise-phase-and-slice-measurability** (hypothesis): Ground-state phase constancy is componentwise. Transferring a modulus occupation bound requires a measurable retained region lying in a common allowed-slice component for almost every bath with positive marginal mass, together with control of the discarded volume. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the complex vector and its modulus have identical occupation.', 'Transferring the modulus bound through disconnected retained regions without a phase estimate.']
- **limit-kinetic-coercivity-domains** (hypothesis): The cube inequality requires the hard-core zero region, and the close-neighbor inequality requires the stated ordering of exclusion and neighbor scales. Their conclusions concern form functions and the specified label subsets. Blocked use: ['Using the cube penalty for functions that remain nonzero at forbidden pair configurations.', 'Using the close-neighbor penalty with scales outside the stated ordering.']
- **limit-normalized-insertion-clearance** (hypothesis): The insertion trial requires a real normalized smooth orbital, a configurationwise bound on nearby old centers and the excluded-volume smallness condition. It gives an admissible trial with an energy upper bound rather than an enlarged-system ground eigenvector. Blocked use: ['Inserting an orbital without checking the local excluded volume on the old support.', 'Treating the trial function as an energy minimizer.']
- **limit-spatial-tail-parameter-order** (hypothesis): Removal gaps and joint spatial bounds use sufficiently large scaled density in the fixed exclusion-density product window. The low-count and close-count parameters must be fixed in their stated order before increasing density. Blocked use: ['Applying the spatial estimates with freely varying cutoff parameters at a fixed density.', 'Replacing a joint prescribed-set estimate by an unsupported independence assumption.']
- **limit-continuous-stationary-path-law** (formal_scope): The stationary path measure uses ground-state endpoint weighting and continuous mutual hard avoidance. Its bridge factorization is conditional on the endpoints and retains the avoidance constraint. Blocked use: ['Claiming that collision checks at sampled times reproduce the exact path measure.', 'Treating the interacting stationary trajectories as independent free Brownian paths.']
- **limit-reversal-and-visit-smallness** (hypothesis): Selected-path deletion requires a nonnegative measurable reversal-invariant functional. The interacting visit estimate additionally requires the displayed smallness condition on its exponent coefficient. Blocked use: ['Applying deletion to a signed or orientation-dependent functional without a new argument.', 'Using the interacting visit moment outside its stated smallness regime.']
- **limit-regularity-and-certificate-thresholds** (hypothesis): Regularity estimates fix the path duration and bounded endpoint region before increasing density. Certificate supply chooses the visitor threshold, then a sufficiently small duration, then the density threshold, and gives an unconditional joint failure bound. Blocked use: ['Assuming uniform cutoff thresholds as duration or endpoint displacement varies without restriction.', 'Adding the full-sample certificate event to the later exposed-data conditioning.']
- **limit-midpoint-averaging-and-endpoint-separation** (counterexample_scope): Bridge collision bounds require regular deterministic obstacles and separated corresponding endpoints. The target midpoint is averaged; prescribing it on the obstacle at the middle time forces a collision. Blocked use: ['Using the averaged collision estimate uniformly at a prescribed midpoint.', 'Removing endpoint separation or obstacle regularity from the replacement estimates.']
- **limit-exact-group-conditioning** (hypothesis): The exposed datum records endpoints, eligibility, allocations and trajectories outside the selected group. Individual cutoffs and frozen-path avoidance remain in each reference law, and mutual avoidance remains in the conditioned product with its normalizer. Blocked use: ['Dropping interaction normalizers because the references form a product.', 'Using unconditional good-cell supply as a bound conditional on arbitrary exposed data.']
- **limit-proposals-use-exposed-data-only** (hypothesis): The proposal target region must be chosen from exposed data and have the stated minimum volume inside the successful target set. Compatibility is proved against deterministic permitted opponents with their recorded endpoints. Blocked use: ['Choosing successful midpoint sets after inspecting other unexposed trajectories.', 'Applying the conditioned collision bound without the success-mass and target-volume lower bounds.']
- **limit-annealed-routes-and-finite-lifts** (hypothesis): Good-route construction requires the joint bad-set probability bound, admissible endpoint pairs, sufficiently large period and sufficiently small bad-site parameter. The encounter moment averages one common retained environment with independent route draws; it is not a guarantee for every fixed environment. Blocked use: ['Replacing the annealed encounter estimate by a pointwise environmental bound.', 'Applying exterior-shell detours to wrapping components without establishing finite lifts.', 'Inferring the encounter theorem from deterministic walk repair alone.']
- **limit-common-bath-and-tag-normalization** (hypothesis): The affinity argument requires a positive-mass common measure absolutely continuous with respect to both comparison measures, with bounded squared likelihoods. Disjoint groups contribute sums of tags with the stated density normalization, and both midpoint assignments retain the same observed bath. Blocked use: ['Comparing independently sampled baths as though they were the common-bath occupation integrand.', 'Replacing a sum of tag contributions by a uniformly sampled tag without changing normalization.', 'Using an affinity lower bound when the common measure has zero mass.']
- **limit-cancellation-compatible-factor-deletion** (hypothesis): Normalizer cancellation requires bounded primary marginal densities, uniform insertion compatibility on individually permitted configurations, compatibility after deleting checks or coordinates, and explicit noninteraction of far factors. Deletion removes path instances and incident checks rather than restoring original trajectories with matching labels. Blocked use: ['Canceling far normalizers based only on a separation assertion for primary labels.', 'Replacing a removed auxiliary coordinate by an original reference trajectory.', 'Checking compatibility only on configurations that already satisfy every pairwise constraint.']
- **limit-geometry-witnesses-and-bath-marginal** (hypothesis): The geometry argument distinguishes small fills from large components, uses distinct bath-particle witnesses with close partners, and averages discarded volume under the actual ground-state bath marginal. The omitted-volume estimate retains a separate periodic-component error. Blocked use: ['Replacing distinct witnesses by repeated appearances of the same particle.', 'Identifying the bath marginal with a ground-state law at a smaller particle number.', 'Discarding the periodic-component error before taking the volume limit.']
- **limit-empty-retained-region-and-infinite-components** (edge_case): When an infinite lifted bad component occurs, the whole torus is discarded and the retained region is empty. Connectivity of retained points then supplies no positive retained-volume assertion. Blocked use: ['Using retained-region connectivity alone to conclude that phase loss is small.', 'Assigning a finite fill to an infinite lifted component by arbitrary truncation.']
- **limit-fix-modulus-gain-before-phase-threshold** (hypothesis): The modulus occupation fraction is fixed before increasing the dilution threshold to reduce geometric phase loss. The final volume threshold is locally uniform in scaled density and independent of the chosen ground vector. Blocked use: ['Changing parameters that determine the modulus gain while attempting to reduce the geometric error.', 'Applying a pointwise density-dependent volume threshold to a varying thermodynamic sequence without local uniformity.']
- **limit-no-finite-certificate-for-main-conclusion** (nonconstructive): The main conclusion quantifies over thermodynamic sequences and continuum ground vectors with existential thresholds. Finite checks of lattice constructions do not decide this occupation theorem or its uniformity over the ground space. Blocked use: ['Reporting a finite route or component test as certification of the full condensation theorem.', 'Presenting existential occupation and dilution constants as numerically calibrated thresholds.']

In particular, both $\\Psi$ and $\\Phi=|\\Psi|$ are full ground eigenfunctions at energy $E_N$. They have smooth representatives in $\\Omega_N$. On every connected component $C$ of $\\Omega_N$, either both vanish identically or $$\\Phi>0,\\qquad \\Psi=e^{i\\vartheta_C}\\Phi$$ for one constant $\\vartheta_C\\in\\mathbb R$. For every real bounded Lipschitz function $u$ on $\\Lambda_K^N$, $$\\label{setup:multiplier}\n q_N[u\\Phi]-E_N\\lVert u\\Phi\\rVert_2^2\n                 =\\int\\Phi^2|\\nabla u|^2.$$ The gradient on the right includes all particle coordinates.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-setup-slices",
          "exact_quote": "For almost every fixed bath $Y$, a function $f\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$ restricts to an element of $H_0^1(S_Y)$, where $$S_Y=\\{x\\in\\Lambda_K:(x,Y)\\in\\Omega_N\\}.$$ The same assertion holds when any nonempty subset of coordinates is left unfixed. For the smooth ground representatives, every connected subset of $S_Y$ has one common phase on its nonzero points, or the wave function vanishes throughout it.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-setup-phase-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\{(x,Y):x\\in U_Y\\}$ be measurable. Suppose that for almost every bath with $m(Y)>0$, the set $U_Y$ is contained in one connected component of $S_Y$, or is empty. Define $$A_Y=\\Lambda_K\\setminus U_Y,\\qquad\n \\delta_{\\rm geom}=\\int m(Y)\\frac{|A_Y|}{V}\\,\\,\\mathrm dY.$$ Then $$\\label{setup:phase-loss}\n B(\\Psi)\\ge B(\\Phi)-4\\sqrt{\\delta_{\\rm geom}}.$$ In particular, if $B(\\Phi)\\ge c_*>0$ and $\\delta_{\\rm geom}\\le c_*^2/64$, then $B(\\Psi)\\ge c_*/2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-componentwise-phase-and-slice-measurability",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "Ground-state phase constancy is componentwise. Transferring a modulus occupation bound requires a measurable retained region lying in a common allowed-slice component for almost every bath with positive marginal mass, together with control of the discarded volume.",
      "support": "stated"

          "anchor": "stmt-sp-cube",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a Euclidean cube of side $l$, and let $F\\in H^1(Q^n)$ vanish almost everywhere whenever $|x_i-x_j|\\le r$ for some $i\\ne j$. With no boundary condition on the outer faces, $$\\label{sp:cube-bound}\n \\sum_{i=1}^n\\int_{Q^n}|\\nabla_iF|^2\n \\ge c_{\\rm cube}\\frac r{l^3}n(n-1)\\int_{Q^n}|F|^2,\n \\qquad c_{\\rm cube}=\\frac1{64\\sqrt3}.$$ Consequently, for every torus form function $F\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$ and every fixed label subset $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}$, $$\\label{sp:cell-energy}\n q_I[F]\\ge c_{\\rm cube}r\\int\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal L}\n                    n_v^I(n_v^I-1)|F|^2.$$ For $D\\ge2$ this implies $$\\label{sp:energy-lower}\n \\frac{E_N}{N}\\ge c_E\\alpha,\n \\qquad c_E=c_{\\rm cube}/2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sp-close-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0<r<s<1$, let $F\\in H_0^1(\\Omega_N)$, and fix a label set $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}$. Define $d_i^I=\\min_{j\\in I\\setminus\\{i\\}}d(x_i,x_j)$, with value $+\\infty$ when the minimizing set is empty. Then $$\\label{sp:close-energy-bound}\n q_I[F]\\ge c_{\\rm near}\\,rs^{-3}\n        \\int\\#\\{i\\in I:d_i^I\\le2s\\}|F|^2,\n \\qquad c_{\\rm near}=\\frac{27}{262144\\sqrt3}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-267.5"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Equation <a href=\"#path:law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"path:law\">[path:law]</a> defines a probability measure. Every time marginal is $\\mu$. Its restriction to an interval of length $s$ has the same construction with endpoint multiplier $e^{sE_N}\\Phi(X_-)\\Phi(X_+)$, and it is invariant under time reversal.         },
        {
          "anchor": "path:law",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu_N$ be the measure on $N$ independent torus Brownian motions of generator $\\Delta$, on $[0,2t]$, with Lebesgue initial measure. Define $$\\label{path:law}\n \\,\\mathrm d\\mathcal R(\\omega)=e^{2tE_N}\\Phi(Y)\\Phi(Z)\n \\mathbf 1_{\\{d(\\omega_i(u),\\omega_j(u))>r\\ \\forall u,\\ i<j\\}}\n \\,\\mathrm d\\nu_N(\\omega).$$ Here and below $d$ is the torus distance.         }

Choose a common uniform ordering of the shared midpoint instances; their physical labels in the two copies may differ.
